# EXPANSION CW120-07 — For Saturday

## A prose-first journey-and-memory story collection about people, rooms, and what the record cannot safely authorize.

### Prose Wave 120: Journeys, Names, and Lessons Left Open

## Batch brief

**Content type:** original game-content proposal with scene, diegetic-record, conversation, and conditional-return drafts.
**Content bank:** 48 distinct beat pairings, each with four alternative authored forms (192 candidate passages).
**Current local anchor:** cassette_fathers_tapes_1/for_saturday — For Saturday.
**Tone:** grounded, human, restrained, material, and careful about what a damaged record can prove.
**Scope:** game content only; no production code, JSON data, route, quest, flag, simulation, or save changes.

## 1. Expansion thesis

For Saturday expands the cited cassette as an authored human record about a kitchen-sounding Saturday tape for Mira that mixes a dripping tap, a washer, soup, school shoes, a later train, and a small request to eat something green. It follows the specific labor, domestic details, names, delays, and small promises in the source, while keeping every interpretation attributed and its unknowns intact.

## 2. Story question

What does For Saturday preserve about a kitchen-sounding Saturday tape for Mira that mixes a dripping tap, a washer, soup, school shoes, a later train, and a small request to eat something green, and what should its listeners leave unresolved?

Source excerpt: A man's voice, home-sounding, kitchen noise behind it: 'Saturday tape, for Mira. The bathroom tap drips — I know, I said I'd fix it, and I'm fixing it. The washer is on the windowsill, so don't throw it out. There's soup on the second shelf and your school shoes dried by the stove; mind the left one, the sole's lifting. I'll be back Sunday, later train, it's all fine.' A spoon taps a pot. 'Eat something green this week. For me. Go on.' ## 5. Human center

The human center is the way a kitchen-sounding Saturday tape for Mira that mixes a dripping tap, a washer, soup, school shoes, a later train, and a small request to eat something green. Keep the concrete detail doing the emotional work: a time, a count, a name, a sound, a household object, or a sentence that the speaker cannot guarantee.

## 6. Voice and point of view

- **Original recorder:** The original recorder knows only the circumstances stated in For Saturday and their own immediate observations.
- **Later listener:** The listener may infer feeling but must not treat inference as a new fact.
- **Present witness:** A present witness responds to one detail without claiming to know the absent speaker’s full story.

Each speaker knows only what the cited source, their own proposed observation, or an explicitly attributed memory could give them. No proposed voice represents a whole faction or settlement.

## 7. Placement and current reachability

The source record verifies a content anchor, not a guaranteed playable route or passage consumer. Before selection, confirm current reachability, content schema, owner, and trigger. Candidate passages may be considered only through a verified existing consumer. Every bank entry remains a candidate, not a promise that all 48 beats occur.

## 8. Player agency

The player may read, ask, carry a sentence forward, leave it where it is, or decline to interpret it. These are editorial postures rather than a promised menu. A refusal or silence is a complete outcome. Any branch that needs runtime state must use the existing owner’s exposed state after verification.

## 9. Continuity, dignity, and safety

Do not invent a new route, quest, faction, schedule, inventory, relationship state, save state, or operational system. Do not convert railway, radio, household, school, safety, medical, or bureaucratic references into player instructions. Keep the speaker’s knowledge, the record’s claims, and proposed interpretation visibly separate. Do not make hazard, scarcity, illness, grief, technical uncertainty, institutional pressure, or memorial language into a puzzle tutorial. Keep proposed identity separate from authored identity and testimony separate from fact.

## 10. Existing hooks and implementation boundary

The candidate content anchor is cassette_fathers_tapes_1/for_saturday, sourced from Assets/StreamingAssets/Data/cassette_sets.json. Verify its current narrative consumer and reachability before placement. This plan creates no new state owner.
The plan creates no parallel authority, new registry, save section, route graph, generic panel callback, or gameplay rule. A selected passage should attach only through a verified existing content owner.

## 11. Narrative sequence

The six movements below organize an editorial bank; they are not six required visits or a fixed quest chain. Beat order may change if the existing consumer calls for a shorter or non-linear presentation.

### Movement 1: Saturday

The tape begins with a day that carries household errands. What makes a day feel ordinary? The movement begins with mira. It stays within the current source boundary and lets the people in the room disagree about meaning without creating a new rule.


### Movement 2: The tap

A promised repair remains part of the message. What does a dripping tap say about distance? The movement begins with saturday tape. It stays within the current source boundary and lets the people in the room disagree about meaning without creating a new rule.


### Movement 3: Washer

An object on the windowsill is protected from being thrown away. Why leave an instruction beside a memory? The movement begins with bathroom tap. It stays within the current source boundary and lets the people in the room disagree about meaning without creating a new rule.


### Movement 4: Soup and shoes

Food and school shoes share a practical inventory. How does care choose its details? The movement begins with washer on the windowsill. It stays within the current source boundary and lets the people in the room disagree about meaning without creating a new rule.


### Movement 5: Sunday, later train

The father locates his return in a changing timetable. What can a promised return sound like? The movement begins with soup on the second shelf. It stays within the current source boundary and lets the people in the room disagree about meaning without creating a new rule.


### Movement 6: Something green

The last request becomes intimate because it is small. Why does the final request stay with us? The movement begins with school shoes. It stays within the current source boundary and lets the people in the room disagree about meaning without creating a new rule.

## 12. Beat bank usage

Each of the 48 pairings below combines one movement with one local motif. The four forms are alternatives: a scene for an observable moment, a record for an attributable fragment, a conversation for disagreement, and a consequence vignette for a verified return condition. Selecting one does not select the others. The bank is deliberately larger than a single quest so writers can choose a truthful scale for the current consumer.

## 13. Player-facing tone

Keep sentences close to material detail. Let people speak in different rhythms: a keeper names what is present, a reader separates record from inference, and a passerby notices what the institution leaves outside its frame. Do not force a moral verdict. A person can decline, wait, misunderstand, or leave a sentence unfinished. The content should make room for a player’s attention instead of replacing it with a score.

## 14. Editorial acceptance

Do not invent a new route, quest, faction, schedule, inventory, relationship state, save state, or operational system. Do not convert railway, radio, household, school, safety, medical, or bureaucratic references into player instructions. Keep the speaker’s knowledge, the record’s claims, and proposed interpretation visibly separate. Before any passage is selected, re-check the source files, current data schema, narrative continuity, and the owner that would display it. Accept only the smallest coherent set of candidates that can be attributed and reached truthfully.

### Editorial scale notes

**01 — source fidelity.** The anchor remains cassette_fathers_tapes_1/for_saturday (For Saturday). Every proposed sentence must stay attached to the cited local record, and every inference must remain visibly an inference. The source can support a human moment without supporting a new mechanic, route, authority, or outcome.

**02 — material attention.** The strongest details in this bank are physical: a threshold, a surface, a line of sight, a document, a marker, a schedule, or a room that still has its old shape. Keep those details ordinary enough to be believed and specific enough to distinguish For Saturday from another location.

**03 — voice separation.** The three roles in this plan do different work. One notices the immediate object, one protects the record’s limits, and one carries the emotional consequence. Do not let all three speak in the same polished register or give one person the authority of the whole For Saturday record.

**04 — player space.** The player is allowed to observe before deciding what the observation means. A pause, refusal, question, or incomplete answer can be the passage’s consequence. Do not turn every emotional beat into a prompt, reward, relationship delta, journal flag, or measurable improvement.

**05 — return scale.** A return to For Saturday should make the earlier passage newly legible, not rewrite it. The later reader may notice a different word, remember a different cost, or decline to repeat the account. The catalog facts remain stable unless a current authorized system says otherwise.

**06 — uncertainty.** Uncertainty is authored content here. It is not a hole that needs to be filled with a hidden culprit, a technical explanation, a secret route, or an invented survivor. When a source leaves a name, cause, or outcome open, the prose can make that openness felt without pretending to solve it.

**07 — dignity.** People associated with For Saturday should not be reduced to the hazard, policy, machine, or record that surrounds them. Let a person have a practical task, a private limit, a bad day, a memory that is incomplete, or a reason not to answer. Keep the human center larger than the prop.

**08 — content scale.** The 48-beat bank is deliberately editorial. A production owner may select one passage, several passages, or none. The size of this document is a writing resource, not a request to implement every candidate or to create a parallel narrative registry.

**09 — continuity check.** Before placement, compare this bank against newer data, especially records that mention cassette_fathers_tapes_1/for_saturday or the surrounding region. Confirm names, truth classes, branch outcomes, and display ownership. A matching phrase is not proof that a passage remains current.

**10 — hazard boundary.** The plan’s boundary is explicit: Do not invent a new route, quest, faction, schedule, inventory, relationship state, save state, or operational system. Do not convert railway, radio, household, school, safety, medical, or bureaucratic references into player instructions. Keep the speaker’s knowledge, the record’s claims, and proposed interpretation visibly separate. Keep that limit visible in scene language. A character may be afraid, cautious, angry, or wrong without the prose becoming an operational guide.

**11 — diegetic surface.** A record, notice, conversation, or scene should look like something a survivor could encounter in the existing content owner. It may be a torn page, a spoken recollection, a label, a pause at a threshold, or a later sentence repeated with one word corrected. It should not look like a system specification inside the fiction.

**12 — emotional movement.** The movement through For Saturday should be felt as a change in attention. The player begins by seeing an object or condition, then hears a person attach meaning to it, then decides whether to carry that meaning forward. Nothing larger is required for the passage to matter.

**13 — open ending.** Close on a human image rather than a verdict. A hand lowers, a page remains folded, a name is repeated once, a room continues its ordinary work, or a landmark stays where it was. The ending should leave room for the player and the current canon to do the next piece of work.

**14 — handoff.** If a content owner accepts a passage, preserve its attribution and keep the owner’s existing state, save, and routing seams. The handoff is complete only when the selected text, its source record, its trigger, and its audience all agree. Otherwise this remains editorial prose for review.

The proposed bank keeps returning to For Saturday because repetition is part of survival writing. A person sees the same surface twice and notices a different cost. That change belongs to attention, not to an invented state.

A selected passage may mention cassette_fathers_tapes_1/for_saturday by its display name, by a local nickname already present in the source, or by an ordinary physical detail. Do not add a second identifier merely to make the prose sound larger.

The strongest callback is often a correction. Someone says a word too confidently, another person supplies the source’s narrower wording, and the room continues. That is enough movement for a scene whose subject is memory.

Keep proposed objects portable in meaning but bounded in authority. A page can be carried, a marker can be seen, and a sentence can be repeated without creating an item registry, a universal rule, or a new save section.

Let silence have more than one cause in the prose: respect, fatigue, uncertainty, refusal, distance, or the simple fact that the current record ends. Never use silence as permission to invent a secret.

Where a source names a hazard, write the people’s relationship to that hazard rather than a method for defeating it. Fear, caution, grief, and disagreement are content; operational guidance belongs to an authorized system owner, not this bank.

Where a source names an institution, show its paperwork and its consequences without making the institution omniscient. A form can be current and still miss a person. A schedule can be obeyed and still be unjust.

The handoff question is simple: can a reviewer point from each important claim to a current source and from each proposed passage to an existing consumer? If either answer is no, keep the passage in editorial review.

The document’s length does not widen the canon. It gives the writer room to choose among small moments at For Saturday while keeping the source boundary visible in every form. A longer bank is useful only when its alternatives remain honest and distinct.

A final reading should sound like a person who has lived with the place, not like a catalog that has learned to emote. Keep the nouns concrete, keep the claims attributed, and let the last sentence leave a little air around the player’s choice.

The last editorial pass should ask whether the passage could belong to someone standing near For Saturday with limited time, imperfect memory, and no need to impress a reviewer. If the answer is yes, the bank has done its work: it has made a place available for play without pretending the place belongs to the plan.

The plan can end with the ordinary world still in motion: weather, work, a person walking back toward a settlement, or a room waiting for its next reader. The content has consequence because somebody noticed and somebody else chose what to do with that notice.

A clean handoff leaves the source visible, the voice distinct, and the ending quiet enough to be revised if the canon moves.

The anchored place remains a place, not a promise.

Its horizon can hold a sentence and let the next person decide what the sentence means.

That is enough for one honest return.

The record remains open.

The next reader may leave it that way.

Editorial pass 01 keeps the same discipline at For Saturday: begin with the cited object, let a person name what it costs, and stop before the sentence becomes an instruction. The passage may carry fatigue, tenderness, anger, embarrassment, or a practical disagreement, but it must not smuggle in a route, a hidden rule, or an outcome the current record does not own. If a later reader hears a different meaning, that difference belongs to the reader and the room. The anchor remains available for attention without becoming property of the plan.

### Scene drafts

#### Scene draft 001 — Mira — Saturday

The proposed scene opens in the saturday movement, where The tape begins with a day that carries household errands. The original recorder notices mira within Saturday before anyone turns it into a conclusion. The tape begins with a day that carries household errands. The mira detail enters this movement as The cassette record titled For Saturday says: A man's voice, home-sounding, kitchen noise behind it: 'Saturday tape, for Mira. The bathroom tap drips — I know, I said I'd fix it, and I'm fixing it. The washer is on the windowsill, so don't throw it out. There's soup on the second shelf and your school shoes dried by the stove; mind the left one, the sole's lifting. I'll be back Sunday, later train, it's all fine.' A spoon taps a pot. 'Eat something green this week. For me. Go on.' The proposed mira passage returns to mira while keeping that voice attributable to the tape, not to a new rule or system.

The Saturday movement stays close to mira within Saturday. An immediate action remains small: a hand rests on a rail, a page is folded, a lamp is watched, a name is repeated, or a person decides not to cross the next line. Nothing in that action creates a new system. The place remains the location record, and the people remain responsible for what they say about it.

The question enters without a menu: “What makes a day feel ordinary? In this movement, what does the source say about mira?” The answer is allowed to belong to the speaker. ## 16. Passage-selection map

**Use a scene** when a verified consumer can stage an observable moment without implying unsafe traversal or a technical procedure. **Use a record** when the game already has an appropriate reader-facing content owner and attribution can be preserved. **Use a conversation** when the passage benefits from two people who disagree in good faith. **Use a consequence vignette** only when the existing route exposes the condition that selects it; otherwise use a non-conditional close or omit the variant. These are editorial selection notes, not proposed runtime features.

## 18. Local-canon and collision audit

The direct anchor cassette_fathers_tapes_1/for_saturday was selected after searching the existing expansion documentation for anchor collisions. This is a bounded content audit, not a claim that no related theme exists anywhere in the project. Search again before implementation. The cited source records above are the canon boundary; all proposed scenes, voices, and artifacts must be checked against any newer narrative data before they are selected.

No real-world country, war, person, copied art, copied text, or real-world interface layout is introduced. The prose is original and specific to the local fictional records.

## 19. Acceptance and handoff

- Keep the 48 beats as an optional content bank; do not implement all 192 candidates by default.
- Preserve current source facts, hazard language, item descriptions, and named-character outcomes.
- Verify a real content consumer and route before selecting a passage.
- Keep new voices and props editorial until an authorized owner accepts them.
- Do not change production code or authoritative game data as part of this plan.
- Review voice distinction, factual boundaries, attribution, and branch handling before content placement.

This file is a complete prose expansion proposal. It contains no implementation claim and no assertion that any candidate passage is already reachable.
